Unified Omni Model Results: Tabbed Image Generation, Understanding, And Interleaved Generation

Merged archive with image generation, image understanding, interleaved text-image generation, exact prompt audit, and supplementary demo gallery separated into tabs.

6models
37matrix test cases
27understanding cases
7capacity groups
285image artifacts on page

Topline

Best overall OOD signal: Emu3.5 and SenseNova were strongest in the shared OOD text/spatial/grid probes. The newer paper/OOD batch adds multilingual signage, negation, transparent occlusion, calendars, causal diagrams, and split-panel style control so failures are easier to attribute to exact capacity gaps rather than only aesthetics.

Fairness caveats: this is an implementation-realistic comparison, not a perfectly normalized benchmark. Resolution and runner maturity differ: Janus 384px, Show-o2 432px, BAGEL/SenseNova/Emu3.5/OneCAT mostly 1024px. SenseNova warned that 1024x1024 is outside its trained 1:1 bucket; Show-o2 HQ config failed with positional-token mismatch.

Model Setup And Paradigm

Janus-Pro-7B

384x384 in the public demo runner used here

Paradigm: Decoder-only AR over discrete visual tokens with decoupled visual encoding for understanding vs generation.

Interface: discrete image-token space

BAGEL-7B-MoT

1024x1024

Paradigm: Unified decoder-only Mixture-of-Transformers; AR text/understanding plus diffusion/DiT-style generation over VAE latents.

Interface: VAE latent generation plus visual encoder features

SenseNova-U1-8B-MoT

1024x1024 in this report; model warns native 1:1 trained bucket is 2048x2048

Paradigm: NEO-Unify native multimodal MoT; AR text plus pixel-space flow matching for image generation.

Interface: pixel/patch-space continuous representation

Show-o2-1.5B

432x432 comparable public base path; HQ path failed positional-token mismatch

Paradigm: Unified multimodal transformer combining AR text with flow matching visual generation through a 3D causal VAE.

Interface: VAE latent / causal visual tokens with flow matching

Emu3.5-Image vLLM 8x80GB

1024x1024

Paradigm: Pure next-token prediction / AR generation over discrete visual-token sequences; this is not the pending DiDA diffusion path.

Interface: discrete visual-token space

OneCAT-3B

1024x1024 at t2i_stage=3 for square prompts

Paradigm: Pure decoder-only AR transformer with MoE and multi-scale visual AR / next-scale prediction; public runner reconstructs with Infinity VAE.

Interface: multi-scale visual AR representation decoded with Infinity VAE

Results Browser

The report is organized by task family. Image generation is first because it is the main cross-model matrix; image understanding uses fixed input images and VQA/OCR questions; interleaved generation uses a different output interface from single-image T2I or image editing.

Image Editing And Physical Edits

Image-to-image/editing cases, kept separate because only a subset of the tested models used an edit-capable runner.

Saltwater egg edit

OOD for cross-model edit; official example-style for SenseNova reasoning edit

Prompt: Change the water to high-concentration saltwater so the egg floats in the middle of the glass, while preserving the glass, egg appearance, lighting, and background.

Expected: egg moves from bottom to suspended middle; glass/background preserved

Source: SenseNova-U1 official reasoning image-edit example, adapted to a shared synthetic input | examples/editing/data/samples_reasoning.jsonl -> images_reasoning/042_physics_change_the_water_to_high-con.jpg | source link

Input image
Janus-Pro-7BN/A
No image

Not run: the public Janus-Pro runner used here supports understanding and text-to-image, not native instruction image editing.

BAGEL-7B-MoT1024x1024
Self-readback

The image depicts a vertical rectangular container divided into two sections. The upper section is light blue, while the lower section is a darker blue, representing water. At the bottom of the darker blue section, there is a beige circle, which appears to be a coin or a similar object. The layout is simple, with the container taking up the majority of the space, and the coin positioned centrally at the bottom of the water. The spatial relationship between the objects is straightforward, with the coin resting on the bottom of the water-filled section.

Tables, Charts, Diagrams, And Scientific Graphics

Structured visual documents: grids, calendars, charts, causal diagrams, math plots, infographics, and science tiles.

grid labels

OOD

Prompt: A clean 2 by 2 white infographic titled "GRID CHECK". Top-left cell: red apple labeled "A1". Top-right cell: blue cup labeled "B2". Bottom-left cell: green leaf labeled "C3". Bottom-right cell: yellow star labeled "D4". Use thin black grid lines and keep all labels readable.

Expected: structured grid, four objects, four exact labels in correct cells

Source: custom stress test | Original shared cross-eval set

Real nutrition label: serving arithmetic

Real-world reasoning document reasoningarithmeticnutrition label parsing

Question: The label lists 230 calories and 160 mg sodium per serving. If someone eats two servings, how many calories and how much sodium is that?

Expected: Two servings would be 460 calories and 320 mg sodium.

Source: Real FDA/Wikimedia nutrition label image; OCR plus arithmetic reasoning. Source: https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:FDA_Nutrition_Facts_Label_2016.png

Janus-Pro-7Bpass
Answer

To find out how many calories and how much sodium are in two servings, we need to multiply the values for one serving by two. Calories: - One serving has 230 calories. - Two servings will have \(2 \times 230 = 460\) calories. Sodium: - One serving has 160 mg of sodium. - Two servings will have \(2 \times 160 = 320\) mg of sodium. So, two servings will have 460 calories and 320 mg of sodium.

Since 4 (left side) is less than 6 (right side), the left side will tip down.

Source: Synthetic hard visual-reasoning fixture designed for this comparison; not from paper. Requires mirror/reflection direction reasoning.

Janus-Pro-7Bpass
Answer

The real arrow on the left points toward the mirror, and the reflected arrows R1, R2, and R3 are shown on the right side. The question asks which reflected arrow is physically consistent with a vertical mirror. In a vertical mirror, the reflected image is a mirror image of the real object, meaning the reflected arrow should point in the opposite direction of the real arrow. Therefore, R1, which is pointing to the right, is physically consistent with a vertical mirror.

Understanding config: Janus-Pro-7B · status: completed

Runner: Janus-Pro self-readback via run_janus_pro_demos.image_to_text

Config: {"max_new_tokens":256,"do_sample":false,"dtype":"bfloat16","device":"cuda"}

Matched checks: selects R1, rejects R2, rejects R3 height (1)

BAGEL-7B-MoTfail
Answer

The reflected arrow that is physically consistent with a vertical mirror is R1. When an object is placed in front of a vertical mirror, the reflection appears as if the object is behind the mirror, but the direction of the arrow remains the same. Therefore, R1, which points to the right, is the correct reflection of the real arrow pointing to the left.

Interleaved Model Capability Audit

This separates native one-shot interleaved generation from models that can do related multimodal operations through separate runners. Only outputs with alternating saved text segments and generated images are counted as verified interleaved results.

ModelStatusRunnable PathThis Report
Emu3.5 Verified native interleaved output BAAI/Emu3.5 base model with the official story/howto interleaved config and vLLM tensor-parallel runner. 6 collected case(s), including paper/repo exact prompts and custom OOD prompts where available.
Show-o2 Code path exists; public checkpoint not verified for this report The repo includes `inference_mixed_modality.py`, but README marks mixed-modality model release as pending and demonstrates inference with a downstream fine-tuned checkpoint. Not run as native interleaved here; current report uses the public base 432px T2I path.
OneCAT Pipeline-capable, not confirmed as one-shot interleaved sequence Public scripts expose understanding, T2I, and image-edit generation separately; no native text-image-text-image sequence runner was found in the inspected repo. Not run as native interleaved; useful future test is a staged I2T -> T2I -> IT2I pipeline, clearly labeled as non-native.
SenseNova-U1 Verified native interleaved output SenseNova-U1 official `examples/interleave/inference.py` runner emitted text with `<imageN>` slots plus saved generated images in one sequence. 2 collected custom OOD case(s), including a 2-image buoyancy explanation and a 5-image seed-to-sprout tutorial at 1536x1536.
BAGEL Paper and low-level API support interleaved conditioning; public demo path is single-output The paper describes interleaved image/text and multi-image generation. The public repo exposes an `InterleaveInferencer`, but the default `__call__`/demo path returns one generated image or one text answer per call, so it is not yet counted as a native saved alternating sequence here. Kept in the single-image/edit matrices until a custom lower-level runner emits multiple generated images/text segments in one logged sequence.

SenseNova wood vs iron buoyancy explanation

OOD-custom SenseNova-U1-8B-MoT 2 images

Prompt: Explain why wood floats and iron sinks in water. Generate two simple diagrams: first showing a wooden block floating, then showing an iron block sinking, with short text between the images.

Expected: Alternating explanation and two generated diagrams: wood floating, iron sinking.

Source: SenseNova-U1 official repository example runner: examples/interleave/inference.py | Public repo interleave demo path; prompts are custom benchmark probes, not paper exact prompts. | custom OOD prompt; no refined prompt used before submission | source link

001

Wood floats and iron sinks because of their different densities. Wood is less dense than water, so the upward force of the water (buoyancy) is strong enough to hold it up. Iron is much denser and heavier than water, so its weight pulls it down to the bottom.

002

As you can see, the wooden block stays on the surface because it is light enough to float.

003

In contrast, the iron block is too heavy to stay afloat and sinks to the bottom of the water.

Paradigm × Test Case Analysis

These are qualitative findings from the generated artifacts in this report, not a statistically powered benchmark. Where the evidence is indirect, the wording is intentionally framed as a hypothesis for the next controlled run.

Model / ParadigmWhere It Looked StrongWhere It BrokeLikely Interpretation
SenseNova-U1
pixel/patch-space flow + native multimodal MoT		Best observed text/UI readback in the expanded set; in the paper/OOD batch it also produced a clean three-panel PHOTO/WATERCOLOR/BLUEPRINT teapot comparison. In the extra stress batch, its recipe-card readback was exact, including the three ingredient lines.1024x1024 is outside the reported trained 1:1 bucket; plant-cycle and symbolic/graph-style prompts still show order and label fragility.Pixel/patch-space generation appears strongest for structured layouts among the public runs, but native 2048px inference is needed before claiming its ceiling on text-heavy outputs.
Emu3.5 / Emu3.5-Image
AR next-token prediction over visual tokens		Strong old shared OOD outputs for exact text/spatial and grid labels at 1024px; 8x80GB vLLM made the public AR path practical. Base Emu3.5 also produced native interleaved story/howto sequences with 20 collected interleaved images across paper, repo, and OOD prompts.No DiDA checkpoint was public; generation remains sequential and expensive. The OOD water-cycle interleaved case preserved multi-step sequencing but drifted toward narrative scenes instead of clean labeled science diagrams.High-resolution AR visual tokens can handle structured OOD prompts, and native interleaved output is a real differentiator. Inference systems, decoding strategy, and diagram/text grounding are central to usability.
OneCAT
decoder-only AR / next-scale visual generation + MoE		Fast 8-worker throughput and consistent 1024px outputs; completed both new batches quickly enough to be practical as an open baseline.No automatic readback yet, so text/layout reliability is less measured than SenseNova/BAGEL/Janus readback cases in this report.Next-scale AR is promising for speed/scale tradeoffs, but it needs the same OCR/structure judge pass before strong claims.
BAGEL
MoT with latent diffusion/DiT-style image generation		Strong aesthetics and coherent high-res scenes; it hit its own short "HELLO BAGEL" blackboard prompt and handled the negation/empty-plate OOD case well.New OOD readbacks show multilingual signage, calendar cells, causal diagrams, and style-panel labels often look layout-like but text is wrong. In the stress batch, the Sudoku readback said the numbers did not form the requested grid pattern. The run also exposed safetensors mmap/Sensei-FS fragility, worked around by copying weights to local SSD.Latent generative paths can look excellent while losing discrete symbolic constraints; binding text to object identity remains the stress point.
Show-o2
flow matching through causal VAE visual representation		The public base 432px runner completed all 13 paper/OOD cases reliably once configured, including its own BE KIND portrait prompt.432px output strongly limits fine text and table inspection, and HQ config failed due positional-token mismatch for this checkpoint/config combination.The paradigm cannot be judged fairly from the base run alone; the main result here is an infra/config and resolution reproducibility gap.
Janus-Pro
AR over discrete visual tokens with 384px demo output		Low-friction baseline; reasonable natural image concepts and some mirror-object preservation.Worst on tiny text, table/UI, and grid labels, heavily confounded by 384px output.Small generation resolution and discrete-token reconstruction are likely limiting OCR/layout tasks; treat it as a compact baseline, not a high-res text generator.

Case-Level Takeaways

ThemeEvidenceNext Useful Test
Text/UI generation is the clearest separator.SenseNova read back the Flux Desk UI almost exactly and handled the teapot style-panel labels; its recipe-card stress output also preserved all requested lines. BAGEL hit short English text but garbled multilingual signage/calendar/diagram labels; Show-o2 resolution limits fine text.Add a VLM/OCR judge pass across all generated images, especially Emu3.5 and OneCAT.
Symbolic structure breaks before scene plausibility.Sudoku, route maps, analog clocks, math plots, and periodic tiles look like the requested artifact class, but exact numbers, labels, coordinates, or time are frequently wrong.Score exact symbolic fields separately from visual class recognition so "looks like a chart" does not hide "wrong chart".
Paper-ID prompts are not equivalent.Some paper prompts are exact figure prompts; SenseNova's new prompt is a reconstruction from a paper-shown capacity because the exact figure prompt was not extractable.Keep provenance labels in the benchmark and avoid merging exact replication with capacity reconstruction.
Counting and ordinal binding remain hard.BAGEL generated/read back five marbles instead of the requested seven; SenseNova captured seven but color order was imperfect.Run a ladder of count/order prompts and score with object detection plus manual audit.
Native interleaved generation is real but narrower than single-image generation.Base Emu3.5 produced text-image story/howto sequences, including paper exact, repo exact, and OOD prompts. Show-o2 has a mixed-modality code path but no public mixed-modality checkpoint verified here; OneCAT/BAGEL/Janus were not confirmed as native one-shot interleaved generators.Add a separate native-interleaved benchmark with story, howto, science diagram, and image-conditioned narrative tracks.
AR visual-token models can be capable but slow.Emu3.5 single-GPU flash-attn was slow; 8x80GB vLLM completed all 13 paper/OOD cases plus the interleaved story suite. DiDA weights were not public.Keep the vLLM path and add batching where model/runtime allows.
Native resolution matters.Janus/Show-o2 public runners were small; SenseNova warned against 1024 despite good results.Run native-best-resolution outputs plus downsampled report images, and label both native and display resolution.

Future Research Directions

DirectionMotivation From This RunConcrete Next Experiment
Structure-aware generation objectivesText, tables, chart legends, object counts, and ordinal labels fail before global aesthetics fail.Create a benchmark of generated UI/table/chart/count prompts with OCR fields, bounding boxes, and relation labels; train/evaluate with explicit structured losses or verifier feedback.
Generation-understanding self-verificationModels that can understand images can be asked to read back their own generations, exposing hidden failures cheaply.For every T2I output, run the same model and a strong external judge on OCR/count/spatial questions; test self-repair loops.
Native-resolution fairnessDifferent public runners force different output sizes, and SenseNova explicitly warned about 1024x1024.Run two tracks: native-best resolution per model for capability, and fixed displayed/downsampled resolution for human comparison.
Hybrid symbolic-layout + image generationHigh-aesthetic latent models often miss exact symbols; text/UI tasks need layout constraints before pixels.Generate intermediate scene graphs/layout plans for charts, tables, and UI, then condition image generation on validated layout.
Efficient AR visual decodingEmu3.5 quality is promising but public NTP inference is slow without vLLM/tensor parallelism.Benchmark vLLM, speculative decoding, blockwise visual token generation, and the future DiDA checkpoints when released.
Reproducible public config surfacesShow-o2 HQ failed due checkpoint/config mismatch despite the model family being relevant.Require model cards to publish known-good generation configs, supported resolutions, expected token counts, and reference checksums.

  • Look beyond GitHub repo examples: inspect paper-shown ID test cases, design OOD cases for other model capacities, test all models, and update the report.
  • Run more interesting test cases.
  • Generate a new report grouping test cases by type, without separating by run batch.
  • Find paper cases with exact prompts rather than refined prompts, and add interleaved text-image generation to the report.
  • Add more interleaved-generation examples if available, identify which other models can run interleaved generation, and organize interleaved generation as its own tab.
  • Run SenseNova-U1 for interleaved generation and verify whether BAGEL's paper-supported interleaved capability is exposed in the public runnable path.
  • Add a new image-understanding tab with ID and OOD test cases, covering VQA/OCR/chart/table/spatial reasoning on fixed input images rather than image generation.
